# T0-35: Countries and Cities for GenAI Careers 1: the Americas, Europe and Israel

S-numbers (S1, S2 and so on) point to rows of the Sources & Evidence Ledger; [S12] means ledger row 12. Grades follow the protocol: A is a primary source opened and read; B an established secondary source or large dataset (Levels.fyi, PwC Worldwide Tax Summaries, Crunchbase News, Indeed Hiring Lab are B); C third-party analysis, a single corroborating source or a user-contributed index (Numbeo is C); D recalled from memory; M my own calculation, which inherits its weakest input; E only in absence records. A slash in a grade cell (for example A/C) means the principal inputs span those grades, strongest first; a composite finding is only as strong as its weakest principal input. The report date is 2026-11-17: sources were retrieved between 2026-11-12 and 2026-11-17, while the brief fixes its anchor at 2026-11-11, so a few values dated 2026-11-12 or 2026-11-13 (Levels.fyi pages, Numbeo, ECB reference rates) fall after the stated evidence window and are used as published. Evidence dated before 2025-01-01 is flagged as possibly stale where it appears. Every table row carries a grade and a ledger reference; the Data Appendix repeats all measures and absences as JSON Lines. Tax and visa figures are pointers and illustrative calculations, not advice.

| P3 | Germany's 2026 EU Blue Card floors are EUR 50,700 and EUR 45,934.20 for shortage and ICT roles (claim K2). | high | D |

| P6 | Indian nationals face a US employment green-card backlog of more than ten years in EB-2 and EB-3. | high | D |

## Research Question

**Decision question.** Which of 19 countries and 29 cities in the Americas, Europe and Israel give a GenAI professional the best mix of job volume, pay after tax and rent, legal access, remote reach and ecosystem depth, and how does the order change for five reader lenses (highest net pay; fastest legal access for an Indian national with a bachelor's degree and five years' experience; best remote reach from India; best long-term settlement path; best for early-career candidates)? The output feeds decisions D-003 and D-007.

**Sub-questions.** (1) Demand: posting counts and AI talent indicators. (2) Headline pay: AI/ML engineer and software-engineer baseline total compensation. (3) Net pay at a reference salary, with regimes for incoming workers. (4) Rent and cost of living, and disposable income after tax and rent. (5) One-line access headline per country with an official source and an India note. (6) Remote reach. (7) Ecosystem. (8) Stability of rules in 2025-2026. (9) Language. (10) A provisional top five per lens, without weighted scores.

**Scope as applied.** All 19 countries and 29 cities in the brief. Two deliberate departures, stated here so maintainers can reverse them. First, the brief asks for the reference salary to be the local senior AI-engineer median; Levels.fyi publishes ML-engineer title medians only for the US (USD 285,650) and the UK (GBP 104,465), so the reference is the senior software-engineer median total compensation of the main city where banked, otherwise the all-level median (flagged in each row), and for the five US hubs the US national senior median of USD 255,000. Second, because reference salaries differ by country, a second comparator applies EUR 100,000 gross everywhere. Mid-level AI/ML engineer pay was not obtainable from one consistent source and is listed under Absences.

**Method.** Sources were read with a fetch tool restricted to pages surfaced by search; pages that returned errors, robots blocks or login walls were recorded as Blocked and not worked around. Pay: Levels.fyi city and country pages (B), n and date recorded. Tax: PwC Worldwide Tax Summaries pages for 19 countries (B), turned into single-person calculators written for this session (employment income only; no dependants, church tax, pension top-ups or privately bought health insurance); parameters that PwC pages did not give are recalled and graded D in each record's note. Currency conversion: ECB euro reference rates of 2026-11-13 [S66] for every cross-country figure; no purchasing-power conversion. Rent: Numbeo (C), converted at the same rates. Access: official government pages, in English where the authority offers it and in the national language otherwise (German, French, Spanish and Portuguese pages were read, plus Polish and Italian pages from advisory firms where no official page was reachable). Time-zone overlap: 09:00-18:00 local against 09:00-18:00 IST on 2026-11-13 and 2026-07-15, computed from the IANA time-zone database. Falsification: (a) a reproduction test of claim K3 against live Levels.fyi pages; (b) a search for evidence against the two access leaders (Canada's Global Talent Stream and Finland's fast-track permit), which found a shrinking referral-partner list and slower LMIA processing in Canada (so, with stated times found for the Netherlands, the UK and Ireland as well, Canada leaves the fastest-access top five) but no 2025-2026 delay evidence for Finland; (c) a search for Estonia's 2026 tax rate that overturned a recalled rise to 24%; (d) re-ranking net pay at the common EUR 100,000 gross, which reorders the middle of the table (see KF3); (e) a cross-check of Indeed's AI-keyword share against PwC's share of postings requiring AI skills, which agree on rank for the eight countries in both (see KF10). Sample sizes are those printed on each Levels.fyi page and are recorded in the data appendix.

## Recommendation

Decision rules for a job seeker, one per lens in section 10 of the Detailed Findings, each with its failure mode. Net-pay figures are order-of-magnitude (about three percentage points of effective rate either way) and are software-engineer based, not ML-engineer based; every route listed needs an employer to file, so none counts the time to find a job.

1. **Highest take-home: United States, Switzerland, Israel, Netherlands (with the 30% ruling), Canada.** Net pay at the reference salary is EUR 146,448 to EUR 168,536 in the five US hubs, EUR 141,125 in Zurich, EUR 99,827 in Tel Aviv, EUR 97,195 in Amsterdam with the ruling and EUR 90,302 in Vancouver (KF2). At a common EUR 100,000 gross the order is Switzerland (EUR 77,623), Estonia, the US, the UK and Israel (KF3), so the US lead comes from pay level, not tax. *Failure mode:* the two best-paid places have the hardest documented gates for an Indian national (wage-weighted lottery, a litigated USD 100,000 payment, EB-2 dates about 13 years behind, a Swiss quota of 8,500); the Dutch ruling shrinks in 2027; stock pay can fall.
2. **Fastest documented access: Finland, Spain, the Netherlands, the United Kingdom, Ireland.** Stated or observed times are two weeks (Finland, fast track), 20 days (Spain, statutory), a two-week target with a 90-day legal period (Netherlands, recognised sponsor), usually three weeks (UK Skilled Worker from outside the UK) and a processing date three weeks behind the page date (Ireland, Critical Skills; a backlog age) (KF8). Canada's Global Talent Stream (a stated 2 to 4 weeks, with slippage) is just outside, as are Denmark and Israel. Germany's Blue Card (EUR 50,700, no lottery, no quota) has no stated time in the sources read and is unranked, not slow. *Failure mode:* these are standards, targets and one backlog date, not measured times, and an entry visa may add a separate step that was not read; the Dutch figure is a target with a 90-day legal period behind it; Finland's market is small; Canada's referral-partner list shrank from 58 to 41 and its LMIA time rose from 10 to 12 business days.
3. **Remote work from India: Finland, Israel, Estonia, the Netherlands, Sweden.** These have 4.5 to 5.5 hours of overlap with an Indian working day (KF9); no US or Canadian hub has any on a standard day. *Failure mode:* overlap is not hiring openness, and no measure of employers hiring people who live abroad was found; the lens is the weakest in the report.
4. **Long-term settlement: Germany, Norway, Ireland, the United Kingdom (Global Talent route), Canada.** Germany settles Blue Card holders after 21 months with German B1 or 27 without; Norway after 3 years; Ireland after 5; UK Global Talent after 3 or 5 (Detailed Findings, sections 5 and 8). *Failure mode:* rules moved in 2025 to 2026 in the UK, Norway and Ireland; Canada's placing rests on category design, not on documented years; Germany's net pay is lower (EUR 58,142 in Berlin at the senior median).
5. **Early career: Germany, Canada, the Netherlands, Ireland, Finland.** Graduate-specific or low entry floors (Germany EUR 45,934.20 for new graduates and the Opportunity Card; Netherlands 30% ruling floor of EUR 36,497 for under-30 master's holders; Ireland EUR 40,904; Finland EUR 3,937 a month) and, for Canada and Munich, entry-pay samples of 352 and 51. *Failure mode:* entry-level GenAI vacancies are scarce and entry pay outside the US rests on small samples (Zurich n=35, Copenhagen n=11); the US and Switzerland have the highest entry medians but the weakest entry-level access.
6. **Before acting,** confirm the threshold on the official page cited in the access table; thresholds change each 1 January in most countries, and the Dutch, UK and US rules above are the most likely to move in 2027.

**What would change these rules:** a ruling on the USD 100,000 payment or a new Visa Bulletin; Dutch legislation for 2027; UK settlement guidance with a start date; Canadian Global Talent Stream or Express Entry changes; consistent GenAI posting counts by city; ML-engineer pay by country from one source; stated processing times for Germany, France, Austria, Switzerland, Norway, Portugal and Poland; official pages for the Italian, Polish and Portuguese thresholds; GenAI-specific demand counts by city.

## Alternatives Considered

| Option | Verdict | Reason |
|---|---|---|
| Weighted composite score per country | Rejected | The brief forbids weighted scores; the knowledge base computes its own ranking from these measures. |
| ML-engineer medians as the reference salary | Rejected for most countries | Levels.fyi gives ML-title medians only for the US and UK; senior software-engineer medians exist for 14 cities and the US national page. ML medians are used for the US and UK as a second comparator. |
| Official tax calculators | Not used | The brief allows published schedules, so PwC Worldwide Tax Summaries (B) plus recalled parameters (D) were used, with each recalled parameter listed; no official calculator was run, so none could check the results. |
| Same gross for every country (EUR 100,000) as the only comparator | Kept as a second comparator | It isolates tax and social contributions but ignores that pay levels differ; the local-reference comparison shows what a person at local market pay keeps. |
| Purchasing-power-parity conversion | Not used | Pay is converted at market rates (ECB); Numbeo's local purchasing-power index (C) is reported alongside, unconverted, for residents' context. |
| Numbeo as the only rent source | Kept | No consistent official rent series for all 29 cities was reachable; every rent figure is grade C and some are older than the evidence window. |
| One platform (Indeed) for postings | Kept, partial | It covers eight countries; platforms differ in definitions, so no cross-platform counts were mixed. |
| Ranking cities within countries | Kept for the five US hubs and the Canadian, German, Spanish, UK and Polish pairs or trios | Net pay and rent differ by state or city; the national senior median is applied to all US hubs because no city-level senior split was banked. |

## Detailed Findings

One subsection per coverage item in the brief, ten in all. Every row is graded and carries ledger references; rows built from my own calculation are grade M and say which inputs they inherit. Pay and tax figures are for a single person and are pointers, not advice.

### 1. Demand and AI talent indicators

GenAI-specific posting counts by country or city were not obtained. Two country-level AI-posting indicators were found, and neither is GenAI-only or a city figure. (a) The Indeed Hiring Lab AI Tracker gives the share of all Indeed postings that contain AI keywords for nine countries, eight of them in scope (Australia is outside the brief); the daily file was re-read in full and matches the banked values. (b) PwC's 2026 Global AI Jobs Barometer (Lightcast data) gives the share of postings that require AI skills in 2025 for 14 of the 19 countries. A share can rise because AI postings grow or because other postings fall, a keyword mention is not a skill requirement, and a posting count is not a count of GenAI roles.

**(a) Indeed Hiring Lab: share of postings containing AI keywords.**

Counts are as the documents give them: stated in the text or labelled on a chart (B), or chart-derived or inferred where the summary of the chart was ambiguous (C: Netherlands, Italy, Switzerland). The United Kingdom, Ireland and Denmark documents state no 2025 total. PwC's global document lists 27 countries and gives no country figures in the text read [S219].

**Cross-checks.** On the eight countries in both sources the rank order agrees (Spearman rho 0.83, n=8; PwC's 2025 share against the mean of Indeed's daily share over 2025; own calculation, grade M [S70] [S220] [S221] [S222] [S223] [S224] [S225] [S226] [S232]). Ireland and Canada are first and second on both, and the Netherlands is the main disagreement (fifth highest of eight on PwC, lowest on Indeed). Canada's PwC share more than doubled from 1.6% in 2024 to 3.5% in 2025 and Denmark's rose from 2.1% to 3.2%, so the rise seen in Indeed's series is not an Indeed-only effect, although Indeed's 2026 levels are not comparable with PwC's 2025 shares. Levels differ between sources: a Lightcast article on the Stanford AI Index gives Spain 3.3% and the US 2.6% (its first takeaway says 2.5% for the US) against PwC's 4% and 2.8% [S217]; an AI World article quoting the same Lightcast data gives about 56,000 postings for Poland and above 60,000 for France against PwC's 78,000 and about 178,000, and its wording implies roughly 76,000 for Germany against PwC's about 125,000 [S218]. These conflicts were not reconciled, so levels are indicative and the rank order is the firmer finding (own judgement).

**Not covered.** Neither source covers Finland, Estonia, Austria or Israel, and Portugal appears only as one of three EU leaders on share (with Poland and Luxembourg, no percentage) in the AI World article, grade C [S218]. City-level counts, GenAI-only counts and AI talent-concentration indicators (Stanford AI Index country charts) were not reachable: see Absences. The pay tables below carry Levels.fyi sample sizes, which show how many salary submissions exist per city but are not a measure of demand.

### 3. Net pay at the reference salary

**Method and limits.** Single person, employment income only, no dependants, church tax or pension top-ups, tax year 2026 where the PwC page gives it (2025 scales for US federal, German, French and Finnish income tax; the basis for each country is in the calculator table), employee social contributions included, privately bought health insurance excluded (this matters for Switzerland, the Netherlands and the US, where individually paid or employer-subsidised health cover is a real cost not deducted here). Reference gross is the senior software-engineer median total compensation where the city or national senior page was banked, otherwise the all-levels median. Total compensation includes stock, which is taxed here as salary and may vest at other times. Currency conversion uses the ECB reference rates of 2026-11-13 [S66]. All rows are grade M; parameters not found on PwC pages were recalled and are grade D (see the calculator table). The calculators were written for this session and are not part of this report; each result record states its inputs and the parameters recalled from memory. Results are order-of-magnitude, about three percentage points of effective rate either way, and Sweden, Finland, Quebec and British Columbia are low-confidence.

Cities with an all-levels reference (London, Cambridge, Dublin, Oslo, Helsinki, Lisbon, Milan, Warsaw, Krakow and Tallinn) understate senior pay in the same city, so their net pay is conservative relative to the senior-based rows. Milan's reference (EUR 35,449, n=18) is too thin to rank on.

**Regime terms as read** (grade is the source's).

| Country | Terms | Src | Grade |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Netherlands | 30% of pay tax-free; salary floor EUR 48,013 (EUR 36,497 for under-30 holders of a master's degree); maximum five years; 27% exempt share from 2027 for people who first used it in 2024 or later. The government.nl page shows 2024 amounts and is stale. | [S170] [S144] | C |
| Denmark | 27% flat tax plus 8% labour-market contribution (32.84% of gross) for up to 7 years; researchers, or key employees with a guaranteed monthly salary of at least DKK 65,400 (2026). | [S129] | A |
| Finland | Key-employee tax at source 25% on gross for people arriving to work in tasks requiring special expertise; tax card valid up to 84 months; no minimum salary stated on the news page. | [S162] | A |
| Spain | Flat 24% up to EUR 600,000 and 47% above; up to six tax years; no Spanish tax residence in the prior five years; the tax agency page gives no rates. | [S171] [S135] | C |
| Portugal | IFICI: 20% flat on Portuguese-source employment income for 10 years; eligible activities include teaching and scientific research; whether an ML-engineer role qualifies was not established. | [S161] | B |
| Italy | Inpatriate regime: 50% of employment income exempt (60% with a minor child), capped at EUR 600,000; four-year commitment; high-qualification requirement; benefit length not read. | [S169] | C |
| France | Impatriate regime: exemption of the impatriation premium or a 30% flat valuation of total pay; no French tax residence in the prior five years; to the eighth calendar year after arrival. | [S143] | A |
| Sweden (not modelled) | Expert relief: 75% of pay taxed; monthly pay above 1.5 times the price base amount (SEK 59,200 in 2026, so about SEK 88,800) unless in research and development; duration stated as both three and seven years. | [S172] | B |

The top-rate column is the headline figure on the PwC quick chart; its scope differs by country (federal only for the US, national only where municipal tax is extra) and it is not like-for-like. Conflicts left unresolved: Ireland's USC band edge (28,700 on one page, 27,700.01 on another; the calculator uses 28,700); Sweden's expert-relief duration (3 or 7 years in different sources); Finland's 2025 state scale is used because the 2026 listing could not be reconciled.

### 4. Living costs, rent and disposable income

Rent is Numbeo's user-contributed average for a one-bedroom flat (grade C; not audited; Numbeo shows some pages in USD or EUR at its own conversion, which was converted to EUR at the ECB rate of 2026-11-13 for the table). Disposable income (own calculation, M, inheriting C) is net pay at the reference salary less twelve months of rent. It excludes all other living costs, social contributions already deducted are not recovered, and it does not reflect employer-paid benefits such as health cover. Boston has no usable rent record.

### 5. Access headline per country, with official sources and an India note

The first table gives the main route for a degree-holder with five years' experience and the entry salary test; the second gives settlement, stated processing times and India-specific points. Official-source rows are grade A (the issuing authority's own page, read in the evidence window); law-firm or news summaries are B or C and marked. Thresholds change each 1 January in most countries, and a page's own statement of amount is used, not an inflation adjustment. India-specific rules were not searched country by country: where the official pages read contain none, the cell says so.

**One-line headline per country, with the official source** (Italy, Poland and Portugal have none read).

| Country | Headline | Official source | Src | Grade |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| United States | Employer-sponsored H-1B through a wage-weighted lottery (the USD 100,000 payment is barred by courts, appeals pending) or O-1A with a petitioner; Indian green-card queue about 13 years. | USCIS; Federal Register; litigation per a law-firm summary | [S34] [S139] [S140] [S35] [S163] | A (litigation: B) |
| Canada | Global Talent Stream needs an LMIA and a contract, about two to four weeks on stated standards; referral partners cut from 58 to 41. | Employment and Social Development Canada | [S36] [S207] | A |
| United Kingdom | Skilled Worker needs a sponsor at GBP 41,700 or more, with a decision usually within 3 weeks when applying from outside the UK; Global Talent needs no job offer; settlement baseline moving to 10 years. | GOV.UK; Home Office | [S118] [S33] [S119] [S211] | A |
| Ireland | Critical Skills permit from EUR 40,904 (since 2026-03-01), Stamp 4 applied for directly, five years to permanent residence; the Critical Skills processing date was about three weeks behind the page date (2026-11-13). | Department of Enterprise, Tourism and Employment | [S121] [S122] [S212] | A (weeks: M) |
| Germany | EU Blue Card from EUR 50,700 (EUR 45,934.20 for shortage roles and new graduates); Opportunity Card at six points; settlement in 21 or 27 months. | Bundesagentur für Arbeit; Make it in Germany; BAMF | [S31] [S32] [S142] | A |

| France | Talent Passport (twice the minimum wage) or EU Blue Card at EUR 59,373, both on a job offer. | France-visas | [S159] | A |
| Switzerland | Quota of 8,500 third-country permits for 2026 with Swiss and EU precedence; degree plus experience. | admin.ch; State Secretariat for Migration | [S124] [S125] | A |

| Finland | Specialist permit at EUR 3,937 a month; two-week fast track. | Finnish Immigration Service | [S130] | A |

The USD 100,000 payment for petitions for workers outside the US (Proclamation 11069, effective 2026-09-21, expiring 2027-09-21) was barred by a California district court on 2026-09-30 and blocked by a Massachusetts ruling on appeal, per a law-firm summary that says to check the docket. | Prevailing-wage level sets lottery weight; no fixed floor; O-1A has none | [S34] [S139] [S140] [S35] | A (court status: B) |

### 10. Provisional top five for each of five lenses

These orders are qualitative judgements from the tables above, not weighted scores, and each lens lists the evidence behind every place and the way it could fail. A place can be missing because a measure was not collected, not because it scored badly; the notes say so where that matters.

**Lens 1: highest net pay** (net income at the reference salary, with incoming-worker regimes where they apply; the EUR 100,000 comparator is shown because it removes pay-level differences).

Stock-based pay can fall. The reference is a national median, not an ML median at a named employer. | [S72] [S178] [S66] [S139] [S35] [S163] | M |

**Lens 2: fastest legal access** for an Indian national with a bachelor's degree and five years' experience. Ordered by the stated or observed time to a decision on the main skilled route. The figures mix a service level, a target, a statutory deadline, a "usually" statement and one backlog date, so near-ties are judgement calls. Germany, France, Austria, Switzerland, Norway, Portugal and Poland have no stated time in the sources read and are unranked, not slow. All of these routes need an employer to file; none counts the time to find one, and whether an entry visa is a separate step after the permit was not read for any country.

The UK Global Talent route needs no job offer but no processing time was collected. [S36] [S207] [S208] [S128] [S160] [S127] [S168] [S136]

**Lens 4: best long-term settlement path.** Ordered by documented years and conditions; countries without a settlement source (Canada beyond Express Entry categories, Netherlands, France, Switzerland, Sweden, Denmark, Finland, Spain, Portugal, Italy, Poland, Estonia, Israel) cannot be placed and Austria is unplaced because the Red-White-Red Card Plus is a residence step, not settlement.

| 1 | Germany | Blue Card holders settle after 21 months with German B1 or 27 months without; 56,300 Blue Cards issued in 2024; EUR 50,700 threshold, no quota. | Requires pension contributions, housing, secured livelihood and basic German; net pay is lower (41.8% total deductions at EUR 99,888); first-period employer dependence. | [S142] [S31] [S167] [S182] | A |
| 2 | Norway | Permanent residence after three years; no fixed minimum salary for the skilled-worker permit. | From 2025-09-01 every requirement must be met on the decision date; Oslo pay data is all-levels only (n=82). | [S131] [S132] [S22] | A |

| 5 | Canada (Express Entry) | 2026 Express Entry categories include researchers and senior managers with Canadian experience; no STEM category is in the release read. | Years to permanent residence and the points cut-offs were not collected, so this placing rests on category design alone; the Global Talent Stream entry route is narrowing. | [S141] [S207] | A (placing: weak) |

For contrast, the United States employment green card for an Indian national is about 13 years behind in EB-2 and 12.8 in EB-3 on the November 2026 bulletin dates (own calculation).

**Lens 5: best for early-career candidates.** Weighs entry-level pay samples against low or graduate-specific entry thresholds and routes without a job offer.

| Rank | Place | Evidence (dates in the tables above) | Failure mode | Src | Grade |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Germany | Blue Card floor EUR 45,934.20 for new graduates, shortage occupations and IT specialists; Opportunity Card (6 points) to look for work; Munich entry median EUR 65,712 (n=51). | Entry sample is thin (Munich n=51; Berlin entry not banked); the Opportunity Card gives search rights, not a job. | [S31] [S32] [S39] | A |
| 2 | Canada | Toronto entry median CAD 100,992 (EUR 64,089, n=352); Vancouver CAD 133,247 (EUR 84,558, n=130); Global Talent Stream and Express Entry categories exist. | LMIA and employer contract required; referral partners fell from 58 to 41; Express Entry has no STEM category in the release read. | [S50] [S60] [S36] [S207] [S141] | B |
| 3 | Netherlands | 30% ruling floor EUR 36,497 for under-30 holders of a master's degree (2026); the highly skilled migrant threshold is lower under 30 but the amount was not read. | Ruling falls to 27% from 2027; floors come from a law-firm page (C); no Amsterdam entry pay was banked. | [S170] [S123] | C |
| 4 | Ireland | Critical Skills floor EUR 40,904 (skills list), five-year route to permanent residence, Dublin all-levels median EUR 110,371 (n=987). | No entry-level pay was banked; floors rise in phases to 2030; rent is high. | [S121] [S122] [S15] | A |
| 5 | Finland | Specialist permit floor EUR 3,937 a month (about EUR 47,200 a year, own calculation), two-week fast track; Helsinki all-levels median EUR 74,890 (n=137). | No entry-level pay banked; small market; Finnish tax parameters are low confidence. | [S130] [S23] [S188] | A/M |

Left out despite the highest entry medians: the United States (national entry median USD 140,000, about EUR 126,674), where a Level I registration enters the weighted lottery once against four times at Level IV, and Switzerland (Zurich entry CHF 92,947, about EUR 100,310, n=35), where the quota is 8,500 permits and Swiss and EU recruits take precedence. [S73] [S139] [S12] [S124]

**Across the lenses** (descriptive, not a score): the Netherlands appears in four of the five tables; Canada, Finland and Ireland appear in three of the five tables; Germany leads on settlement and early-career access but not on pay; the two best-paid places (United States, Switzerland) are the hardest to enter on documented gates. No place leads on every lens, so the choice depends on which constraint binds first.

## Open Questions & Risks

### Strongest objections an expert would raise

| # | Objection | Evidence for | Evidence against or limits | Status | Grade of the main evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Levels.fyi medians are self-submitted, skew towards large technology employers and include stock, so using them as reference salaries overstates typical GenAI pay and flatters the US. | Single platform; stock included and taxed as salary; no second source was obtained (TechPays pages were blocked). The brief asked for pay to be corroborated across two sources. | Large samples (US senior n=21,691; Bay Area all levels n=11,785); the ML-title median is close to the SWE median in the UK; sample sizes are large for the US, Canadian, UK, German and Dutch pages. | Unresolved: pay is single-source (B). | B |
| 2 | Calculators built from PwC summaries and recalled parameters mis-state tax for complex systems (Swiss cantons, Spanish regions, Quebec, Swedish municipalities, Finland's 2026 scale). | Recalled parameters are listed per country; Finland uses the 2025 state scale; Sweden, Finland, Quebec and British Columbia are labelled low confidence; no official calculator was run. | Bands come from PwC pages for 2026 in most countries; results at EUR 100,000 gross (22% to 43% total deductions) fall in plausible ranges; the method is single person, employment income only. | Open: treat net pay as within about three percentage points. | B/D |
| 3 | A median salary is not what an Indian hire will be paid, and an ML role may pay differently from a software role. | ML-title medians exist only for the US (about 43% above the US all-levels SWE median) and UK (level with London SWE); mid-level AI/ML pay was not obtained. | Entry and senior medians are shown separately for many cities. | Open. | B |
| 4 | Disposable income after rent ignores health insurance, childcare, relocation, visa fees, remittances and employer-paid benefits, and Numbeo rents are user-contributed. | Two rent pages are older than the evidence window (Lisbon 2023-06, Madrid 2024-06); Numbeo converts some pages at its own rate; Boston has no rent record; Swiss and Dutch health premiums are not deducted. | Every city page is dated in the table; a EUR 300 monthly rent error moves disposable income by EUR 3,600 a year (about 2% of net pay in the US hubs, 5% in Amsterdam and 11% in Lisbon). | Open. | C |
| 5 | The fastest-access ranking uses service standards, targets and one backlog date, not measured times, and real times may be longer. | Disconfirming search for Canada found referral partners cut from 58 to 41 (2026-09-15) and LMIA processing at 12 business days in February 2026 against 10 in November 2025; the Dutch page separates a 2-week target from a 90-day legal period; Ireland's figure is the age of a backlog date. | Search for Finnish delays in 2025 to 2026 found only an alert dated 2019-10-14, outside the window; Spain's 20 days is statutory; the UK page says a decision usually comes within 3 weeks. | Tested: Canada weakened and left the top five once the Netherlands, the UK and Ireland had stated times; Finland not contradicted. | B |
| 6 | Incoming-worker regimes may not apply to a GenAI engineer. | Portugal's IFICI lists teaching and scientific research among eligible activities, and an ML-engineer role was not shown to qualify; Sweden's relief needs monthly pay near SEK 88,800, above the Stockholm senior median; Dutch rules change in 2027. | The Dutch 30% ruling and Danish and Finnish schemes are broad employee schemes on the pages read. | Open: eligibility is not established for Portugal and Finland. | B/C |

The PwC and Indeed shares agree on rank for the eight countries in both (rho 0.83, n=8) and are shown for context. | Open: check live postings before acting. | B |

### Unresolved source conflicts

- Ireland's USC band edge: EUR 28,700 on one page and EUR 27,700.01 on another; the calculator uses EUR 28,700.
- Sweden's expert-relief duration: three years in one passage and seven in another of the same document, and the monthly threshold reading (SEK 88,800 inferred from 1.5 times the price base amount).
- Italy's Blue Card threshold: about EUR 36,300 on a law-firm page modified 2026-06-10, described there as a rise from about EUR 35,500 (the figure The Local gave for 2024); the basis differs between sources (1.5 times the average gross salary per the Italy Handbook, C; the collective-agreement minimum plus the industry average per The Local, B); no Italian government page was read [S214] [S167] [S168].
- Spain's national highly qualified route: the order read earlier was summarised as giving it 80% of the Blue Card threshold, while the Ministry's June 2026 document states EUR 41,356.36 with no reduced amount for that route and ties EUR 33,085.09 to the Blue Card in listed cases; the document's amounts are used [S134] [S216].
- Portugal's Blue Card threshold: EUR 21,030 as a fixed amount, while the same page's FAQ calls it a multiple of national salary or of the Social Support Index; low confidence [S215].
- AI-posting levels: PwC's 2025 shares and counts differ from Lightcast's own article on the Stanford AI Index (Spain 4% against 3.3%; US 2.8% against 2.6%, and 2.5% in the same article) and from an AI World article (Poland 78,000 against 56,000; France about 178,000 against 'above 60k'; Germany about 125,000 against a figure implied at roughly 76,000); PwC's figures are used and the rest are shown as conflicts [S217] [S218] [S224] [S226] [S231] [S233].
- Dutch 30% ruling: a 2024-amount government page against a 2026 law-firm page; the later page is used.
- ML-engineer title median read as USD 285,650 and USD 285,800 on two fetches of the same page; the first is used.
- ECB reference-rate file date: 2026-11-13 is used for every conversion.

### Risks to using this report

- Thresholds and rates change on 1 January in most countries; the Dutch, UK and US rules above are the most likely to change in 2027.
- The Retrieved column in the ledger shows 2026-11-14 for rows S1 to S209 because the ledger helper stamped that date; those pages were read between 2026-11-12 and 2026-11-16, so treat it as approximate to within a few days and rely on each page's own date. Rows S210 onwards were read on 2026-11-17 and carry that date.
- Many pages were read through a summarising fetch tool; figures that mattered were re-read where possible, but a mis-transcribed digit cannot be ruled out. Pay and tax figures should be checked on the source page before a decision.
- Net-pay figures do not include employer benefits, bonuses taxed differently from salary, stock-vesting timing, social contributions above statutory minimums (for example French supplementary schemes) or pension top-ups.

## Discovered Concerns

- **The weighted H-1B lottery penalises entry level.** A Level I registration enters once and a Level IV registration four times from the FY 2027 season; an early-career applicant from India has the weakest position in the US route [S139].
- **A litigated payment makes US cost uncertain in both directions.** Courts barred the USD 100,000 payment on 2026-09-30 while a separate DHS fee proposal of USD 103,265 is not final; a ruling either way would change the cost of hiring from abroad by up to about USD 100,000 per petition [S35].
- **Indeed's AI-posting shares rose several-fold in 21 months** (Canada from 3.64% to 18.97%, the US from 2.71% to 7.34%). That could be a surge in AI postings or a change in keyword coverage; the tracker's definition was not checked, so trends should not be read as hiring growth. PwC's separate measure also roughly doubled in Canada (1.6% in 2024 to 3.5% in 2025), so the rise is not unique to Indeed's series, but the two measures are not comparable in level [S70] [S221].
- **EF EPI 2026 is due on 2026-11-18**, the day after this report's date, and will supersede the 2025 scores used here; the scores come from a Wikipedia compilation, not EF's own table [S152] [S153].
- **The UK Global Talent page read is an archived copy** last updated 2024-10-21; fees and endorsement rules need a live check [S33].
- **Italy issued 633 EU Blue Cards in 2024 against 56,300 in Germany**, a large gap in route use that the sources do not explain [S167].
- **A time-zone database quirk:** the IANA copy used (version 2026e) holds British Columbia at UTC-7 all year from March 2026, so Vancouver's offset to India is the same in November and July; the overlap with India is zero either way [S206].
- **Portugal's reported Blue Card floor is an unverified outlier.** EUR 21,030 is the lowest amount in the access tables, about 41% of Germany's EUR 50,700 (own calculation), and rests on one secondary page whose own FAQ describes the threshold differently [S215] [S31].
- **Anthropic's own announcements are used as evidence for Anthropic offices**, marked COI; a reader who wants an independent view of lab presence should check local press and planning records [S149] [S150] [S151].

## Data Appendix

Records are JSON Lines of two types, `measure` and `absence`; every record has a grade, ledger references (empty only for absences), an as_of date and, for measures, a freshness class (ttl). Subjects use `country:XX` and `city:CC-Name`; a few `region:` subjects mark multi-country figures.

